the two bounded convolution operators.  The substantive open step is to
prove the hypothesis for the explicit product
\CGeqref{eq:RVM-transport-product}.  This hypothesis already has nearly the
full analytic strength of the desired conclusion and must not be advertised
as an easier consequence of the bounded displacement.  The earlier shortcut of testing the operator on $f\equiv1$ is not valid.
Although $f\equiv1$ lies in $\mathcal B_\varepsilon$ and its \emph{normalized
unilateral} transform is $1$, the convolution hypothesis is formulated on
the multiplicative group and the norm of $\mathcal B_\varepsilon$ only controls $x\ge1$, while the
zero extension is an explicit support convention.  Boundedness of that operator on this supremum space does
not, by itself, identify a holomorphic Mellin multiplier in the half-plane.
To deduce holomorphy of $\mathscr T_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ and its inverse from
operator boundedness one would need an additional Mellin spectral-multiplier
theorem, including compatibility with the imposed $0<x<1$ support convention.  Such a theorem is not assumed
silently here.

Thus \CGeqref{eq:RVM-pointwise-two-way-transfer} is a precise sufficient
transfer statement, but proving its hypothesis is itself an RH-strength
problem about the explicit multiplier.  In particular it is not implied by
the known estimate $\gamma_k-\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}=O(1)$, because a bounded
ordinate shift produces the phase factor
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:RVM-phase-shift-size}
 x^{\beta_k+i\gamma_k}-x^{\beta_k+i\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}}
 =x^{\beta_k+i\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}}
 \left(e^{i(\gamma_k-\gamma_k^{\rm RVM})\log x}-1\right),
\end{equation}
whose parenthesis can have modulus $2$.

There is also a rigorous Hilbert-space version.  Suppose ordinary functions
$M$ and $M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ vanish for $0<x<1$, belong to the
displayed weighted $L^2$ space, and realize the transform identity in the
Mellin--Plancherel sense.  Suppose further that, on a line
$\Re s=\sigma$, one has
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:RVM-L2-multiplier-bounds}
 0<c_\sigma\le
 |\mathscr T_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(\sigma+it)|
 \le C_\sigma<\infty
 \qquad(t\in\mathbb R).
\end{equation}
For the normalized transform one has
\begin{equation}
 \int_1^\infty|M(x)|^2x^{-2\sigma-1}\,dx
 =\frac1{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}
 \frac{|\widehat M(\sigma+it)|^2}{|\sigma+it|^2}\,dt,
\end{equation}
and the identical formula for $M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$.  Thus the factor
$1/|s|^2$ required by normalized Mellin--Plancherel is explicit.  Using
\CGeqref{eq:RVM-umbilical-transform}, it cancels on the two sides of the
multiplier comparison and gives
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:RVM-L2-energy-transfer}
 c_\sigma^2\int_1^\infty|M(x)|^2x^{-2\sigma-1}\,dx
 \le
 \int_1^\infty|M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(x)|^2x^{-2\sigma-1}\,dx
 \le
 C_\sigma^2\int_1^\infty|M(x)|^2x^{-2\sigma-1}\,dx.
\end{equation}
This is Plancherel applied to
$e^{-\sigma y}M(e^y)$ and
$e^{-\sigma y}M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(e^y)$ on $y\ge0$.  Estimate
\CGeqref{eq:RVM-L2-multiplier-bounds} is again a hypothesis, not a
consequence of the pole locations.

\subsection{The residue-preserving model and the scope of the excursion theory}

For comparison only, if all zeros are simple and a residue series is given a
specified summation procedure, one may form
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:RVM-residue-model}
 M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord,res}(x)
 =Q_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord,res}(x)+2\Re\sum_{k\ge1}
 \frac{x^{\beta_k+i\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}}}
 {(\beta_k+i\gamma_k^{\rm RVM})\zeta'(\rho_k)}.
\end{equation}
It has the same transported pole locations but generally different residues
from $M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$.  Thus both models have the same pole obstruction,
while only $M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ has the exact multiplier identity
\CGeqref{eq:RVM-umbilical-transform} and avoids $\zeta'$.

Finally, the theorems of this paper split into two classes.  Mellin
continuation, pole obstructions, weighted $L^2$ arguments, and abstract
energy criteria can be formulated for $M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ once their analytic hypotheses
are verified.  In contrast, the excursion identities using jumps in
$\{-1,0,1\}$, zero runs, the divisor recursion, the Catalan selector, and
the arithmetic orientation fibre are properties of the genuine Mertens
path.  They do not transfer merely from
\CGeqref{eq:RVM-umbilical-transform}.  The transported baseline is therefore
an analytic control and a possible multiplier route to RH, not a replacement
for the integer excursion path.


\section{Conclusions}
\label{sec:conclusions}

\subsection{Status ledger}
The conclusions use the same four-way classification as the introduction.

\emph{1. Exact algebraic identities.}  The excursion decomposition, bridge
and Fej\'er identities, prefix/channel formulae, reflection-fibre selection,
finite RVM inversion, exact symmetric Hadamard normalization, the
route-independent distributional endpoint formula, the true-$\beta$ transport
identity and Riesz/energy identities are exact statements about the objects defined in the paper.
This class also includes the consecutive-block transport identity in Audit
proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-quadratic-transport} and the exact normalization
defect $C_{\rm RVM}(\kappa)$ in Audit proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-log-interpolation}.

\emph{2. Theorems proved using published results.}  The horizon localization
and return bounds use Kaczorowski--Pintz after the transform verification in
Lemma~\CGref{lem:KP-M0-transform}; the exact logarithmic exponents use
Pintz~\cite{Pintz2026}; and the short-interval and squarefree-pair
consequences use the stated Matom\"aki--Ter\"av\"ainen and Mirsky theorems
with their strict exponent ranges.  The quadratic-displacement asymptotic
$A_{\rm RVM}^{\rm tr}(T)\sim T\log\log T/(\pi\log T)$ uses Selberg's
unconditional second moment of $S(t)$; the density-one logarithmic
interpolation then follows from that asymptotic and the explicit Taylor
majorant.

\emph{3. Equivalent criteria encoding genuine zeros.}  The endpoint identity
$\zeta_1=\zeta$ of the critical-line ordinate homotopy, the true-$\beta$ RVM
energy criteria, the Gaussian transported-divisor criterion, and the
Hamburger endpoint condition (D) are exact equivalences only after the
genuine zero divisor or its ordinates have been inserted.  They reorganize
the RH obstruction but do not constitute an independent proof of RH.
The fixed real part $1/2$ in the synthetic RVM construction is not itself an
RH assumption: if RH fails, the critical-line endpoint is
$\zeta_{\rm line}\ne\zeta$.  The equivalence becomes an RH premise only if
the endpoint equality is invoked without an independent proof.

\emph{4. Open hypotheses and route-completion targets.}  These include the
one-excursion sublinear first-passage estimate, the multiscale Fej\'er upper
bound, the quantitative Lambert-clock square-root and unit-cell estimates, the
uniform stationary-phase remainder for the new ordinate-homotopy expansion,
the continuous-to-atomic endpoint problem for $\zeta_{\rm line}$, positivity of
the sampled RVM weights, the weighted-variation estimate for the integer-step
defect together with its Mellin--Stieltjes grid correction, the asserted
vertical limit $T_{\rm RVM}$, and the
unsmoothed bidirectional Mellin multiplier
theorem.  Numerical data remain evidence about finite objects only.

Accordingly, the manuscript does not claim a new unconditional proof of RH,
nor a new unconditional RH equivalence independent of the genuine zero
divisor.  Its rigorous contribution is the collection of exact excursion
identities and reductions, together with the consequences obtained from
published oscillation and short-interval theorems; the RVM section separates
those results from its still-open analytic continuation and inversion tasks.


\subsection{What the paper does}
The paper replaces the single function $M$ by the sequence of its
excursions, and asks which statements about $M$ can be read, and which can
be localized, excursion by excursion. Three facts make this possible. First,
an excursion is a bridge: it starts and ends at $0$, so its quadratic mass
is a lag-weighted sum of Möbius correlations internal to it
(Lemma~\CGref{lem:triangular-corr}), and its height and length bound its linear and
quadratic masses sharply (Proposition~\CGref{prop:sharp-LH}). Second,
excursions are disjoint intervals, and disjoint intervals that grow by a
factor $\lambda$ are few: at most $\log X/\log\lambda+O(1)$ of them start
below $X$ (Lemma~\CGref{lem:linear-lacunary}). Third, the oscillation theorems
of Kaczorowski--Pintz and Pintz give, respectively, the number and the
exponent of the large values of $M$. Every main result is obtained by
combining two of these three facts.

\subsection{Horizon localization, area and arithmetic selection}
The horizon-localization theorem (Theorem~\CGref{thm:horizon}) uses the
extremal-frequency form of Kaczorowski--Pintz. If RH is false, the relevant
frequency parameter is the first ordinate $\gamma_\Theta$ on the extremal
boundary (or $\infty$ if the boundary is not attained). Since all zeros up
to $T_0=3\cdot10^{12}$ are on the critical line, the contradiction argument
has at least $(T_0/\pi-o(1))\log X$ large-witness excursions. Consequently
RH is already decided on every fixed linear horizon $\lambda x_0$ with
\begin{equation*}
\lambda>\lambda_0:=e^{\pi/T_0}.
\tag{S18.1}
\end{equation*}
The older $\lambda_*=e^{\pi/\gamma_1}$ is retained as the uniform benchmark
coming from Corollary~4 of \cite{KP86}; it is not the best explicit linear
factor once the extremal theorem and the verified zero height are used. The
corresponding prefix dependency cutoff is $\chi_0=\lambda_0/2$.

The surplus and first-arrival calculations remain structural. They identify
how the disjoint-interval budget, record retrogression and centered adaptive
correlations encode the position of a witness, but they are no longer needed
merely to pass below the uniform benchmark $\lambda_*$.  Proposition~\CGref{prop:centered-log-cost}
sharpens the centered record calculation in the genuinely linear regime by
combining its mandatory logarithmic contribution with half of the exact
record-return cost. Proposition~\CGref{prop:post-horizon-centered-cost}
turns post-horizon chord deviation into logarithmic surplus, while
Proposition~\CGref{prop:three-source-surplus} shows that witness overshoot,
return tail and inter-bad gaps all draw from the same critical logarithmic
budget.  The drifted-walk reference calculation indicates that neither
centered normalization is expected to close the KP-scale problem merely by
its typical size.

The exact area exponent (Corollary~\CGref{cor:area-exponent}) identifies the
growth of the skeleton area as $x^{1+\Theta/2+o(1)}$. The upper bound is a
concavity argument; the lower bound needs the inequality
$B_1\le2(m^*)^{1/2}\mathcal A_{\rm abs}(v^*)$, the exponent of $B_1$ from
Pintz's theorem and the return localization of
Corollary~\CGref{cor:KP-local-returns}. Together these give that the area bound
$x^{5/4+\eps}$ is equivalent to RH.

The analysis of the localized window (Section~\CGref{sec:prefix}) shows that
the RH-equivalent part of an excursion is an explicit linear function of
$\mu$ on the prefix up to $\chi u$, $\chi>\chi_0=\frac12e^{\pi/T_0}$; it
decomposes this function into scale channels, proves a channel-descent
inequality with an explicit geometric loss, and splits $M$ into a smooth part
and a large-prime part which, in the range of the split considered, have size
$(1+o(1))t/\log^2t$ and opposite signs
(Proposition~\CGref{prop:both_parts_large}).

The weighted correlation identity at zeros, combined with averaged Chowla and
with Pintz's exponent (Theorem~\CGref{thm:theta-lag-localization}), locates the
signed weighted correlation total representing $\sum_{n<v}M(n)^2$ at lags larger than $v^\alpha$
for every $\alpha<\Theta$. On the reflection fibre
(Section~\CGref{sec:arithmetic-selection-fibre}), divisor equations reduce the
$2^J$ orientations compatible with the profile $M^2$ to at most $2N$, while
Theorem~\CGref{thm:linear-observability-obstruction} shows that a fixed-shift
correlation cannot be controlled uniformly by $o(N)$ divisor rows.

\subsection{Structural identities}
The skeleton and its area, energy and inflection formulas
(Sections~\CGref{sec:definitions}--\CGref{sec:formulas}), the bridge identity
$Q_\nu=-\sum_hhC_h^{(\nu)}$ with its sharp bounds, the four counting lemmas
for disjoint intervals (Lemmas~\CGref{lem:power-lacunary}--\CGref{lem:linear-lacunary}),
the dipole identity (Proposition~\CGref{prop:dipoles}) and the positivity of the
excursion blocks $D_I(\varsigma)$ each follow in a
few lines from the definitions. Their role is structural: they turn
statements about $M$ into statements about a sequence of disjoint bridges,
to which counting and the oscillation theorems can be applied.

\subsection{Equivalent criteria and transported estimates}
The $L^1$, $L^2$, energy and correlation criteria
(Proposition~\CGref{prop:L1}, Theorems~\CGref{thm:corr-criterion} and
\CGref{thm:energy}) are equivalent to Littlewood's criterion; here they are
derived from the excursion decomposition, and the energy criterion is
squeezed between the $L^1$ mass and the maximum. The exponents of
$\sum|M|$ and $\sum M^2$ are immediate consequences of Pintz's theorem. The
linear window identity follows from $\mu*1=\delta$, and the bilinear
identity is structurally analogous to the $k=2$ Heath--Brown/Vaughan truncation pattern. The results on
correlations in excursion blocks transport published short-interval and
averaged-Chowla theorems to intervals selected by $M$; the additional ingredient specific to the present formulation is that the selected intervals are completed excursions.

\subsection{The pure critical RVM arithmetic system and the critical-line path}
Section~\CGref{sec:RVM-pole-transport} separates a genuinely synthetic arithmetic system from both the critical-line endpoint path and the true-$\beta$ transport.  The midpoint Lambert clock defines $P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm crit}$ and $\zeta_{\rm RVM}$ with nontrivial zeros exactly $1/2\pm i\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}$; the critical real part is an input, not a conclusion about $\zeta$.  The explicit formula defines $J_{\rm RVM}$, and Theorem~\CGref{thm:RVM-path-J0-uniform} proves that its zero sum converges uniformly on compact $x$-ranges.  Finite Möbius inversion defines $\pi_{\rm RVM}$, while Hypothesis~\CGref{hyp:RVM-J-baseline} now records only the stronger quantitative square-root target.  Under the stated unit-cell and Mellin--Stieltjes identification hypotheses, sampling $\pi_{\rm RVM}$ on the integers gives the weights $c_n^{\rm RVM}$ and an integer-supported Euler object with the same zero divisor in $\Re s>0$ up to a nonvanishing holomorphic factor.  The remaining step needed for a positive $0/1$ integer-prime realization is the cell-remainder Hypothesis~\CGref{hyp:RVM-cell-remainder}; it is not silently assumed.  On the Mertens side, $\widehat M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm crit}=1/\zeta_{\rm RVM}$ has pole abscissa $1/2$ by construction and is related exactly to $M$ by the quotient $\mathscr Q_{\rm RVM}=\zeta/\zeta_{\rm RVM}$.  The integer prime defect $E_{\rm RVM}(N)=\pi(N)-\pi_{\rm RVM}(N)$ gives the corresponding exact prime-side comparison.

The critical-line homotopy of Subsection~\CGref{subsec:RVM-critical-line-homotopy} moves $\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}$ continuously to the genuine ordinates $\gamma_k$ while keeping every nontrivial zero on $\Re s=1/2$.  The family preserves the functional equation, pole and trivial-zero structure, and its explicit-formula side is $C^1$ in $\mathcal D'$.  The RVM endpoint is an ordinary continuous function; the genuine-ordinate endpoint has a precise convergence obstruction $D(T;u)$, and under the endpoint premise $\mathbf P$ it agrees with the classical prime-power count away from its jumps.  Proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-path-route-independent} shows that the smoothed endpoint difference depends only on $N_\zeta-N_{\rm RVM}$ and not on the chosen path.  Theorem~\CGref{thm:RVM-path-endpoint-identification} states explicitly that $\mathbf P$ is equivalent to RH, so this chain is a Category~3 reformulation rather than a hidden proof.  The stationary-phase resonance formula is kept formal until a remainder uniform in the harmonic index and in $x$ is supplied.

The monotone matching of the two ordinate lists has an additional
unconditional consequence.  Audit proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-quadratic-transport}
proves, with multiplicities and endpoint errors included,
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}\le T}
 (\gamma_k-\gamma_k^{\rm RVM})^2
 \sim\frac{T\log\log T}{\pi\log T}.
\tag{S18.RVM}
\end{equation*}
Audit proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-log-interpolation} then shows that the
path logarithm is asymptotically affine outside a set of natural density
zero only after the deterministic correction $C_{\rm RVM}(\kappa)$ is
subtracted.  The uncorrected normalized product is not an exact logarithmic
geodesic.  Under $\mathbf P$ and the separately assumed vertical limit for
$\zeta_{\rm RVM}$, Audit proposition~\CGref{prop:RVM-rigidity-loss} gives
loss of vertical boundedness for every fixed $\kappa>0$.  This last statement
is conditional on both premises and is not an unconditional RH argument.

\subsection{The rigid-ordinate transported baseline}
Section~\CGref{sec:RVM-pole-transport} introduces a second, analytic use of
the Riemann--von Mangoldt clock.  Its canonical product $P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ retains the
ordered real parts $\beta_k$ and multiplicities of the genuine zeros and
replaces only $\gamma_k$ by $\gamma_k^{\rm RVM}$.  The identity
\begin{equation*}
 \widehat M_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}=\frac{G}{P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}}
 =\mathscr T_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}\widehat M
\tag{S18.2}
\end{equation*}
is exact, so the relation to the genuine Mertens function is not inferred
from numerical similarity.  The nontrivial pole abscissa remains $\Theta$,
and $P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(s)=P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(1-s)$ holds exactly under RH.  With an oriented assignment
inside every split functional pair, the real-axis reflection defect is
nonnegative and vanishes exactly under RH.  Independently of that
orientation, the Riesz measure of $\log|P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}|$ recovers the nonnegative local
energy $E_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(T)=\sum(\beta_k-1/2)^2$ exactly.  Its vanishing in every window
is equivalent to RH, whereas $E_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}(T)=o(\log T)$ is equivalent to Lindel\"of
through Backlund's local zero-density criterion.
The Poisson-smoothed resonant energy
$\mathcal R_{\rm RVM,a}^{\rm ord}(T)$ is sublogarithmic exactly in the same regime.  It
controls the vertical comparison with the critical rigid reference and
shows why a bounded real-axis comparison does not extend uniformly past a
moving layer containing $\asymp\log T$ rigid ordinates per unit height.

The local Poisson model explains why the critical-line rigid product has a
regular decaying corrugation and why finitely many displaced pairs create
cusps and amplitude modulation.  A single fixed modulus cut measures only
the distance $|\varsigma-\beta_k|$ and cannot recover its side; an alternating
pair $1/2+d,1/2-d$ also gives a regular doubled cell with
$\Theta=1/2+d$.  Two cuts remove that ambiguity, while the complete surface
recovers the horizontal divisor through its logarithmic singularities.
These observations diagnose a given $P_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ but do not independently bound
its real parts.

Subject to the explicit finite-order vertical-growth hypotheses in Theorem~\CGref{thm:RVM-Gaussian-RH}, a fixed Gaussian regularization gives an RH-equivalent divisor criterion.  The corresponding unsmoothed equivalence is reduced to a
bidirectional Mellin-multiplier theorem for $\mathscr T_{\rm RVM}^{\rm ord}$ and its
inverse.  That hypothesis is explicitly recognized as an RH-strength
analytic assertion: operator boundedness alone does not permit the earlier shortcut based on the constant function; a separate Mellin spectral-multiplier theorem would be required to infer half-plane holomorphy.  The
section distinguishes this unproved upper-transfer estimate from the
unconditional lower obstruction supplied by the poles.

\subsection{Limits of the method and open directions}
Several negative results delimit the excursion mechanisms: pointwise channel
descent loses a fixed factor per step, termwise contraction of the exact
kernel gives nothing beyond the unit-step cone, the relaxed divisor class
admits ballistic paths, and geometric inputs alone do not force positional
surplus.  The verified-height refinement also shows that the fixed linear
constant is no longer the principal bottleneck: under failure of RH the
extremal Kaczorowski--Pintz density and the verified height $T_0$ reduce the
linear horizon to every fixed $\lambda>\lambda_0=e^{\pi/T_0}$.

The main forward direction is therefore the one-excursion problem.  A
completed excursion contains more information than an open prefix because
it has one sign until its first return to zero.  Proposition~\CGref{prop:past-forcing}
and Corollary~\CGref{cor:past-sign-coherence} turn this into the exact
past-only quantity
\begin{equation*}
 P_u(t)=\sum_{k\le q_u(t)}M(t/k),\qquad
 \sigma P_u(t)=\sum_{k\le q_u(t)}|M(t/k)|\quad(u<t<v).
\tag{S18.3}
\end{equation*}
Thus the future excursion is constrained by the past before $u$ together
with the no-crossing inequalities and the closing equation $M(v)=0$.
Remark~\CGref{rem:complete-excursion-cone} expresses the same fact as a
linear sign cone for the divisor recursion.  This is the preferred route to
a sublinear first-passage theorem: first characterize the feasible geometry
of one complete excursion, then extend it to all of $M$ by the exact
segmentation into completed excursions and zero runs.

A concrete target is to fix $\theta_{\rm sub}<1$ and a Littlewood-scale level
$u^{1/2+\varepsilon}$, and to show that the relaxed sign cone of a completed
excursion cannot keep
\begin{equation*}
 1\le \sigma M(u+r)<u^{1/2+\varepsilon}
 \qquad(1\le r\le u^{\theta_{\rm sub}})
\tag{S18.4}
\end{equation*}
while also satisfying the later no-crossing constraints and the return
$M(v)=0$, unless some explicitly identified arithmetic constraint on the
past fails.  Because all path inequalities are linear in the relaxed past,
Farkas duality or related convex certificates provide a natural finite
framework: either the linear relaxation already forces an early high level,
or a dual feasible obstruction identifies exactly which arithmetic rows,
zero-mask constraints or scale relations are missing.  This route retains
the complete excursion rather than compressing it to a single energy.

The corresponding first-passage target is now explicit.  For some
$\varepsilon_{\rm L}>0$ and
$1/2+\varepsilon_{\rm L}<\theta_{\rm sub}<1$, one would need an unbounded
sequence of excursion starts satisfying
\begin{equation*}
 \sum_{r\le u^{\theta_{\rm sub}}}M(u+r)^2
 >
 u^{\theta_{\rm sub}+1+2\varepsilon_{\rm L}}.
\tag{S18.5}
\end{equation*}
Lemma~\CGref{lem:prefix-energy-extraction} then forces a value of size
$u^{1/2+\varepsilon_{\rm L}}$ inside the sublinear prefix.  What is not
proved is the production of this \emph{prefix} energy from a later KP
witness; local persistence around a late peak does not transport itself
back to the start of the excursion.  Equation
\CGeqref{eq:open-prefix-energy-corr} identifies the exact signed
correlation-plus-boundary quantity that would have to be controlled, and
the boundary vanishes only when the complete excursion is used.

The centered record functionals remain useful diagnostics of witness
position.  In particular,
\begin{equation*}
\mathfrak C(u,H)=-\sum_{j<H}\frac1{a_j}\sum_{g<L_j}gC_{j,g}^\circ
\tag{S18.6}
\end{equation*}
satisfies both the finite bootstrap
\begin{equation*}
 \log\frac{t_H}{u+H}\le4\mathfrak C(u,H)
\tag{S18.7}
\end{equation*}
and, in the genuinely linear first-arrival regime,
\begin{equation*}
\mathfrak C(u,H)\ge\frac13\log\frac{t_H}{u}
+\frac{t_H-u}{2t_H}-o(1).
\tag{S18.8}
\end{equation*}
For a sublinear Littlewood-level first passage the required scale is much
smaller:
\begin{equation*}
 \mathfrak C(u,H_{\rm L})\ll u^{\theta_{\rm sub}-1}.
\tag{S18.9}
\end{equation*}
The post-horizon quantity $\mathcal W_\lambda$ must also be interpreted
through its intrinsic factorization
\begin{equation*}
 \mathcal W_\lambda
 =\frac Ru\left(\frac{D_\lambda}{H}\right)^2\mathcal E_\lambda;
\tag{S18.10}
\end{equation*}
small $\mathcal W_\lambda$ may mean a short remaining segment rather than an
almost affine rise.  The three-source logarithmic budget remains a useful
packing identity, but none of these scalar observables supplies the missing
sublinear exponent by itself.

A second route retains all dyadic Fej\'er scales of a completed excursion
simultaneously. Proposition~\CGref{prop:multiscale-fejer} uses the one-sign
bridge condition to close the zero-padded path into a cycle with a favorable
boundary sign and proves
\begin{equation*}
 H^2\le m_{\rm dy}\bigl(m_{\rm dy}q+2\mathfrak K_I\bigr).
\tag{S18.11}
\end{equation*}
Thus, under a violation of RH, the linearly short KP witness excursions
selected by the counting argument must satisfy the superlinear lower bound
\CGeqref{eq:RH-violation-multiscale}. This identifies a single signed
collective quantity spanning all dyadic scales. The missing arithmetic input
is exactly the quasi-linear upper target
\CGeqref{eq:multiscale-fejer-target}; it is not supplied by the classical
Fej\'er identity or by the present short-interval estimates.

The logarithmic phase picture has a more limited role.  A formal global zero
mode contributes $k^{-\rho}=k^{-\beta}e^{-i\gamma\log k}$ to the $k$th
channel, and Euler--Maclaurin makes the rotation
$e^{-i\gamma\log K}$ explicit.  This explains the transition
$K^{1-\beta}\asymp|\gamma|$ in a truncated zero-mode channel sum, but it is
global zero analysis, not an additional constraint on a completed excursion.
In particular, $\sum k^{-\rho}=\zeta(\rho)=0$ is not an ordinary convergent
series in the critical strip; it is an analytic-continuation statement.
The useful version inside the method is instead Proposition~\CGref{prop:channel-dirichlet-transform},
which is a finite transform of the actual channel vector of one excursion.
The weighted polynomial
\begin{equation*}
 \mathfrak P_K(\varsigma,\tau;A)
 =\sum_{k=2}^{K}A_k k^{-\varsigma}e^{-i\tau\log k}
\tag{S18.12}
\end{equation*}
can test signed channel cancellation for coefficients $A_k$ generated by the
excursion.  A square-function estimate for these structured coefficients
would be genuinely new, but Remark~\CGref{rem:Ak-channel-polynomial} records
why no such estimate is available for arbitrary coefficients or frequencies.

The global explicit-formula route is therefore retained only as a diagnostic.
At the smoothing orders used by Kaczorowski--Pintz the spectral denominator
suppresses nonleading singularities; returning to bounded smoothing order
exposes the full residue structure (for simple zeros, coefficients of the
shape $1/(\rho\zeta'(\rho))$).  The present excursion programme deliberately
does not assume control of those coefficients.  Its next proof target is
instead local and deterministic: characterize the complete no-crossing
excursion from its past and its own early part strongly enough to force a
sublinear first passage, and only afterwards use the global excursion
segmentation to recover a statement for all $M$.
